# Enhanced Windows mode

Approved design, settled owner decisions, and phase 0 contracts (2026-11-15).
This page supersedes the frontend recommendations in [modern-frontend.md](modern-frontend.md).
The component and action catalogs below are retained in full. Organisation and native
UI choices are ours; the evidence labels distinguish observations from hypotheses.

Phase 0 APIs, implemented routes, limitations and executable checks are documented in
[the contract guide](enhanced-mode/contracts.md). The opt-in [phase 1 native world](enhanced-mode/phase1.md)
uses immutable shipped assets and semantic snapshots. No docking workspace is implemented yet.
Investigation references under `build/workers/enhanced-design/` are ignored scratch,
including every game-art screenshot and resource-derived inventory. Recipes and small
text traces cited here are retained under `evidence/enhanced-mode/`; rerun recipes in
a scratch directory with local verified game files to reproduce omitted captures.
The investigation's historical write restrictions describe that investigation only.

# What the owner wants

Enhanced Windows mode is a **fully native, Photoshop-style workspace for the one recovered game**. The main window contains and organizes game panels; it is not itself a world-only view or the old DOS screen. World views normally occupy the central document region. Overview, Minimap, Behavior, Caste, History, Info/cards, Status, Help, the two command panels and the other inventoried roles become native panels/content that can dock at edges, split regions, stack as tabs, float as owned windows, collapse and return through saved layouts, presets and Reset. Source-modal tasks retain the visibility and input restrictions their waits require.

Deconstruction means understanding **individual components before designing controls**: every DOS Obj, every code-drawn graph/overlay/text region, each Win16 equivalent and difference, its displayed state, interactions, feedback, eligibility and resulting logic. It does not mean wrapping the old widgets, scaling their pixels, or replacing each Obj with a native lookalike. The new interface is organized around an action catalog: modern controls express player intent and invoke the same complete canonical operation, with its preconditions and side effects. Where the operation is inseparable from an original tracker/menu/modal loop, keep that execution hidden and drive it faithfully until a stronger boundary is proved.

The internal simulation logic remains authoritative and unchanged. The port adapts to it; it does not carry another implementation of ants, colonies, tools, scoring, allocation conversion or world generation. Original artwork can be decoded as immutable native assets, but no canonical VGA screen/window/popup is presented in enhanced mode. Classic mode remains the DOS presentation. Panel layout, native focus, extra views and repaint cadence are presentation state; source logical-open/front state remains an engine state machine. The verification target is **action equivalence** and **passive invariance**, independently of workspace arrangement.

This supersedes the earlier world-only-main-window and widget-binding recommendations. The retained research below remains evidence, not a requirement to reproduce 1991/1993 controls. Workspace docking, multiple regions/tabs/floats, saved layouts and two default pinned command panels are settled requirements, not open questions.

Read the report in three passes: the two confirmed bugs and scope limits below; the [component deconstruction](#component-level-deconstruction-window-by-window) and [action catalog](#action-catalog-reinvent-controls-around-canonical-operations) for implementation contracts; then the [workspace model](#workspace-panel-instances-and-logical-window-ownership), [settled decisions](#settled-owner-decisions) and [verification plan](#verification-action-equivalence-and-passive-invariance). The historical inventory and ribbon tables retain earlier research after those new contracts.

# What exists now and why it falls short

## Scope, provenance and evidence strength

Read-only investigation on 2026-11-15. All investigation writes, copied resources, executables, traces and captures are confined to this directory. No canonical source, port source, tracked documentation, reference repository or oracle was edited. No build, promotion, repository gate, commit, push or DOSBox recording was run. Those workflows would violate this task's write boundary. Concurrent clock-worker modifications were observed and left alone.

The executable was copied from `build/current/portable` before capture, with its DLL and BIOS-font resources. Its recorded identity is:

* SHA-256 `2bc5d3675832ad4f9f1e1231fee0962b43bcaddc509d02ce0abd3b92a7d97b7e`.
* Build source revision `5a9ddfa2f05340173a81baad8bfe56a5ec07cc59`.
* Build input hash `f11c44e578284699e184169d17e6603e32d5af18aa06dd2d984ceb2266850086`.

See [build identities](../evidence/enhanced-mode/build-identities.json), [retained build ID](../build/workers/enhanced-design/p/simant-build-id.txt), [initial capture runs](../build/workers/enhanced-design/capture-report.json), [additional runs](../build/workers/enhanced-design/extra-capture-report.json), [tool runs](../build/workers/enhanced-design/tools-capture-report.json), and each run's input script/stderr. Source line numbers in GDB output refer to that build's generated C, not a newly rebuilt clock rule. The native runs used disposable copies of game resources and explicit diagnostics destinations here.

Evidence labels in this document:

* **Observed:** a retained capture or read-only debugger observation on that executable.
* **Source fact:** a named canonical/port function or original-resource field read in this investigation.
* **Reference fact:** a Win16 binary/symbol/resource fact or a Mac resource fact; not automatically a DOS contract.
* **Recommendation:** proposed work, not an implemented feature or a recovered original design.
* **Unsettled:** not established by the inspected evidence. This label is never a request to guess or to change canonical code.

The source census is [resource-inventory.json](../build/workers/enhanced-design/resource-inventory.json), generated by [inspect_sources.py](../evidence/enhanced-mode/inspect_sources.py). It decodes the DOS and Win16 databases and reads the pinned Mac application's resource fork directly from the CD image. [win16-binary-audit.json](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-binary-audit.json) independently records the EXE, SYM, HLP identities, NE resources/imports and relevant MAPSYM entries. Its EXE hash is `538fed3f0fd60a6528a5e5b20990361ac4d274c58266a81e71c24f751612589d`.

The requested Mac `build/workers/mac-scroll/RESULT.md` **does not exist in the supplied checkout**. The Mac README, asset inventory, pinned ISO, resource parser/tooling, MENU/WIND/DLOG/DITL/CNTL and tutorial strings were inspected instead. Mac scroll-loop details not proved by those inputs remain unsettled. The earlier captures are the current port plus a clearly labelled classic SDL capture. The later Win16 runtime observation below supplements the source/resource research; the Mac executable was not run. Neither is a DOS-oracle screen comparison.

## The retained hybrid comparison

The following describes the historical comparison path. The phase 1 preview replaces
the complete Game Window and yard clients; see [implementation and limits](enhanced-mode/phase1.md).

`native_windows.c::ensure_window` creates an SDL window for each hosted logical game window. A native caption replaces part of the game's title strip; `upload_surface` converts hosted VGA planes into a client texture. `render_game_window` first draws that texture and then covers the map area with `modern_game_view_render`. Thus even the modern Game Window retains the old control column and DOS grow decoration. Every other ordinary view, list, triangle, dialog and popup is still a VGA client in a native frame.

`native_menu.c` is a real Win32 menu, built from the DOS menu resource. Its commands enter the original `0xFDxx` event space. The root remains the old logical desktop, cropped below the old menu and scaled by the classic host. It can be a large empty cyan rectangle behind several game windows. Native ownership was requested but this runtime logged `That operation is not supported` for several windows. That is an observed limitation of this environment/path, not proof of correct owned-tool-window behavior on another desktop.

The modern map is closer to the desired direction: it reads world cells through `modern/world_view.c`, composes their sprites using a pure compositor and uses its own camera. However, it still depends on canonical sprite-bank residency and the VGA terrain cache. Spider cells and status text are copied from hosted pixels. Balloons, mower outlines and some help overlays are missing. This is unsuitable as the foundation of a fully native UI without changing those dependencies.

## Bug 1: ants disappear during the birth dialog — confirmed causal chain

**Observed reproduction:** [birth-modal input](../evidence/enhanced-mode/dialog-trace/birth-modal/input.txt), [GDB commands](../evidence/enhanced-mode/dialog-trace/birth-modal/gdb.txt), [observer](../evidence/enhanced-mode/dialog-trace/birth-modal/observe.py), [complete trace](../evidence/enhanced-mode/dialog-trace/birth-modal/gdb-out.txt). The recipe starts Quick Game, types the game's existing `erad` cheat through ordinary keyboard input to reduce health to 1, lets normal death/rebirth run, and dismisses preceding prompts. It does not call game functions or write game memory from the debugger. The cheat is a bounded way to reach the real birth path, not a proposed product feature.

1. `src/sound/game_interface.c::myBeginSong` deliberately calls `edit_release_life_tiles()` before loading a song when the sound interface and music option are enabled. This historical memory-management policy is legitimate game behavior.
2. The trace's backtraces show `YellowDeath -> myBeginSong(song=20007) -> edit_release_life_tiles`, then `YellowBirth -> myBeginSong(song=11005) -> edit_release_life_tiles`.
3. At entry to `YellowDialog`, `MapPlane=2`, `MapPnt={28,0}`, both `edit_life_tiles_handle` and `edit_life_alt_tiles_handle` are null, but `LifeA/B/R` contain respectively **2 / 2 / 4 nonzero cells**. The ants have not all been removed from the simulation.
4. `world_view.c::life_record` reads those temporary handles. If the selected bank is absent, `modern_world_cell_pixels` successfully returns the **ground tile only**. `modern_world_cell_key` includes a one-bit bank-presence flag, so the modern cache actively replaces previously complete cells with ground-only cells when the bank disappears.
5. `YellowDialog` opens logical `0x1A00` and enters its original timing/input waits. At the 40,000 ms capture, the outer-loop counter is still 163, both handles remain null and the life counts remain 2 / 2 / 4. The renderer can continue presenting during that wait, but the ordinary edit draw has not prepared the bank again.
6. After dismissal, normal `edit_render_viewport -> edit_prepare_life_tiles(MapPlane/2)` restores the necessary bank. The longer [death-birth trace](../evidence/enhanced-mode/dialog-trace/death-birth/gdb-out.txt) ends with the nest handle present again.

[Scratch image: Actual rebirth dialog](../build/workers/enhanced-design/dialog-trace/birth-modal/frame.bmp.1A00.png)

[Scratch image: Modern world during rebirth: ground remains, ant imagery disappears](../build/workers/enhanced-design/dialog-trace/birth-modal/frame.bmp.0000.png)

The same moment's [hidden canonical edit image](../build/workers/enhanced-design/dialog-trace/birth-modal/frame.bmp.0000.bmp.hosted.png) still contains the last drawn player ant. The disappearing native sprite is therefore not a canonical instruction to erase it.

This is a **renderer asset-lifetime error exposed by music and a modal wait**, not an empty life map or a need to simulate through the dialog. `win_EditChanged` also invalidates the canonical viewport cache on window-state changes; that cache is not the world model and must not become the replacement renderer's authority. Hosted-plane overlays introduce additional independent risks, but are not needed to explain this reproduction.

**Correct design:** native-owned, immutable terrain/sprite/illustration assets decoded from the verified shipped resource inputs, and a coherent world snapshot independent of canonical cache handles, clip lists and raster planes. The native renderer never loads/pins a bank in the canonical resource cache, calls `edit_prepare_life_tiles`, forces `UpdateEdit`, disables music, or treats “resource temporarily not resident” as “entity absent.” A missing required native asset is a startup/resource error. A modal wait retains the last complete world snapshot or publishes the unchanged current world with those assets. Dialog visibility cannot affect entity visibility.

## Bug 2: several clicks to use another game window — confirmed gates, bounded attribution

The present design explicitly implements the historical **raise-and-eat** convention:

* `native_windows_translate` checks the last presented `w->view.top`. On button-down in a background logical window it records `eaten_button`, queues `raise_pending`, and consumes the down. It consumes the matching up too. A modal logical top blocks the request.
* `pump -> sim_window_hosting_raise -> win_ToTop -> win_Open` performs logical activation later. `win_Open` rebuilds pointer regions, draws, and **flushes the canonical event queue**.
* `src/window/events.c::win_translate_pending_event` rejects a normal object event if its high-byte logical owner differs from `clip_window_z_order[0]`. `win_IsWinInFront` is exactly that logical-order comparison; it does not ask Windows which HWND has keyboard focus.
* Native activation, SDL focus/capture, the last presented logical-top snapshot, source hot regions and canonical event delivery are therefore separate authorities. `SDL_RaiseWindow` is deliberately configured not to activate on show/raise. A held pointer is also retargeted through `window_under` in the current adapter; that behavior is special to the old proximity-menu tracking design and must not govern native control dragging.
* `run_windows.py` and `windows-caste.txt` explicitly test that a first click **does not** select Manual and a second does. This unwanted extra click is intentional in the current adapter, not speculative Windows behavior.

The source establishes why at least an activation click is lost and why already queued following inputs can be lost or rejected. The precise number of extra clicks on the owner's physical desktop was not measured; stale presentation state, queue flush timing and OS activation/capture can compound it. Do not claim a specific OS mouse-activation defect without that trace.

**Correct design:** native focus is real focus. An enabled nonmodal control accepts the activation click. Queue one durable **semantic action intent** outside the canonical queue. At a valid source seam, use its audited complete logic entry when available; otherwise validate the modal owner, prepare its canonical logical owner once, let the source flush complete, and deliver the retained event/transaction exactly once. Observe the resulting state rather than the previous render's `view.top`. Do not simply stop eating the SDL click: that still races the flush and front-window rejection. No automatic click retry, timer sleep, direct state write or forced dispatch around source modality.

For sliders/triangles, retain the complete down/move/up transaction and pointer ownership until the source tracker completes. Docking/native window activation never synthesizes a gameplay click. Switching between controls inside the two native panels normally requires no OS-window activation at all.

## Additional observed presentation gaps

The original Game Window help labels exist in the [hosted image](../build/workers/enhanced-design/r2/help/frame.bmp.0000.bmp.hosted.png) but are covered in the [modern image](../build/workers/enhanced-design/r2/help/frame.bmp.0000.png). This follows directly from drawing the hosted client first and the modern world over it. The native design needs semantic help content and anchoring, not another overlay copied from the old image.

The map/yard control rows retain their own DOS title and grow decorations in some hosted captures. The native root/menu capture and Game Window capture are available in [native-probe](../build/workers/enhanced-design/native-probe/result.json). These `PrintWindow` captures were made by a DPI-unaware observer and crop part of the game client; use the complete renderer captures for content. A later attempt to obtain DPI-aware popup captures did not complete before the task steering; its incomplete directory is not screenshot evidence. Desktop-wide `ImageGrab` failed in this session. No composited desktop screenshot is claimed.

## Supplementary Win16 runtime observation

Both `D:/Games/ANTWIN/` and `D:/Games/wrun/` contain byte-identical SIMANTW.EXE, SIMANTW.SYM and SIMANT.HLP, checked in [win16-reference-identities.json](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-reference-identities.json). `D:/Games/wrun` contains portable `otvdmw.exe`/`otvdm.exe` (winevdm); `C:/tools/CATALOG.md` was checked as requested. The runner README documents its redirected Windows directory. The runtime, DLLs, game and profile were copied under this output directory, and `WINDIR`/temporary/profile destinations were set here. No installation/registry-registration tool or DOSBox was run.

The original Win16 binary ran under the copied interpreter. [Initial runtime report](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-observation/result.json) records AntRoot, RibbonWindow and GenericWindow children; [title](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-observation/advance-0/05.png), [chooser](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-observation/new-game/05.png) and [later registration capture](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-controls/advance-0/05.png) retain actual GDI content. These are interpreter-on-current-Windows observations, not a complete Windows 3.1 machine recreation. PrintWindow/DPI differences leave unpainted margins in some captures; they are not presented as image-accuracy proof.

The follow-up [control probe](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-controls/result.json) did **not** finish a valid Quick Game transition: registration/chooser windows remained active. Sending subsequent menu commands produced a runner #GP during the Caste probe; that run was stopped and all its windows were closed. Do not infer gameplay behavior, control eligibility, or a game defect from that invalid-state probe. No successful History/Info/Save As runtime observation is claimed. Their component descriptions below rely on the recovered original code, resources and symbol/binary audit. The successful title/chooser/native host observations and the failed probe are both retained so the limits are reproducible.

Further ordinary-input trials are retained in [live attempt 1](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-live/result.json), [live attempt 2](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-live2/result.json) and [message-input attempt](../build/workers/enhanced-design/win16-live3/result.json). Waiting for title creation was necessary; the real-input attempt did not advance the title, while window messages advanced to registration but did not dismiss it. These trials therefore add no valid gameplay/control observation. Each runner was closed and reported no remaining windows. They are input/runner investigation limits, not evidence of original game defects.

Recovered original code makes the useful distinction: Win16 hosted each game logical window as a native child and supplied native menu/frame/edit-scrollbar/file-dialog facilities, but much game content—including triangles, bars, picture buttons, graphs and proximity menus—remained custom GDI/Obj drawing. The new workspace adopts the separation of host and game semantics, not the old widget implementation.

# Component-level deconstruction, window by window

This section comes before the action catalog deliberately: identify each resource component and each code-drawn component, then choose modern controls around its meaning. The following tables cover **all 285 DOS Obj records**, including noninteractive frames, pictures, styles and unknown regions. They do not prescribe copying those widgets. [component-deconstruction.json](../build/workers/enhanced-design/component-deconstruction.json) retains raw initial type, flags, group, layout operands and payload metadata alongside each interpretation.

**Common source rules used explicitly in every row:** visible flag 0001 and selectable flag0002 are separate; 0004 is selected, 0008 toggles, 0010 proximity, 0020 exclusive group, 0400 sticky selection, 0800 click feedback. The source has no universal separate enabled boolean inferred here. Runtime group flags, modal owner and action preconditions win over resource defaults. A normal object event can be rejected unless its owner is canonical front. Generic source click classification can create double-click bits within ten ticks; application code may add its own waits. A native component gets its own normal focus/keyboard/hover feedback; only actual source actions request source sound/cursor/messages.

**Reading the Win16 column:** resource-slot metadata is a verified structural comparison, not automatic proof of purpose. Named ribbon and handler mappings are stronger semantic evidence; unknown correspondences remain marked. WINGANT is the color resource set; MWINNT is independently retained in the inventory as the monochrome alternative. Most shared content remains custom GDI/Obj drawing in Win16. Native window frames, menu bars, scrollbars and native file dialogs are called out separately. Exact observed screenshots are limited to the states noted in the runtime reference section.

**Shared component mechanics (source facts):** `src/window/events.c::win_dispatch_object_event` applies a five-countdown click flash for visible nontoggle feedback controls; exclusive/sticky selected toggles resist deselection. It classifies repeated same-object/matching-modifier clicks within ten source ticks. Lists are dispatched to `list_process_click`; sliders to `win_ProcSliderEvent`; caption types12/18 can start source window movement only when the window title-drag flag permits it. `win_step_object_focus` moves the source pointer among selectable front-window objects, rather than maintaining a modern keyboard focus tree. Reinvented controls use native focus and accessibility; an E/T action still preserves the source effects it requires.

`src/window/lists_sliders.c` selects a row using font-height/list-top arithmetic. Type8 is a linked list scrollbar: absent link or count<=visible returns immediately; top/bottom arrows repeat while StillDown, modifier mask6800 jumps to an end. The inspected handler has no thumb-drag/page-track branch. `src/window/object_attributes.c::win_DoProxMenu` opens the menu, seeds an optional proximity item, runs ButtonHeldInit/ButtonHeld plus win_GetEvent, commits an accepted in-range object/proximity release, then closes and returns its index or -1. It is not a generic Windows popup with an assumed Return/Escape contract. `StillDown` in menu_helpers also treats Insert/Space and Delete scan states as mouse buttons. Native key bindings must drive only the intended action transcript; raw workspace keystrokes cannot leak into these trackers.

## Win16-only ribbon and programmable-button components

These **95 additional Win16 Obj records** are decomposed individually as reference components. They are not extra DOS logic. Each is custom game/GDI content in the RibbonWindow/GenericWindow host unless native platform chrome is explicitly stated. The two ribbon definitions share one HWND in the original; the new workspace can keep two independent semantic command panels visible. Initial flag/group metadata is recorded exactly, and normal Win16 source modal/front/selection rules still apply.

| Win16 component / type / initial flags / group | Displays / state and interactions | Trigger / feedback / availability | Modern counterpart |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2200** frame/fill(0), flags0800, group0: ribbon or chooser container | resource object state; original source frame/lifetime | Exact source flags determine visibility/proximity/selection; source action determines cursor/sound/message and mode restrictions. [H02](#action-h02) | native panel/container |
| **2201** picture(6), flags0801, group0: ribbon/chooser illustration or background | resource object state; no separately established business action | Exact source flags determine visibility/proximity/selection; source action determines cursor/sound/message and mode restrictions. no gameplay action | native layout/decoded illustration |

### Win16 native controls outside the game Obj resources

* Native edit scrollbars: WS_HSCROLL/WS_VSCROLL when record flag8 permits a sizing frame; WM_HSCROLL/VSCROLL reaches DoEditScroll only for logical Edit. Line operations repeat while left button is down. Modern replacement is explicit N03 pan and H05 zoom, no world scrollbar. Native frame/system-menu Close/Next/Zoom are reference K04; modern docking/focus uses H01-H03.
* Native OPEN dialog: Static400 “Open File Name”, Edit401, Static402 “Files in”, ListBox404, Static403 path, Button1 Open and Button2 Cancel. SAVEAS: Static400 label, Edit401, Static405 Directory label, Static402 Directories, ListBox404, Static403 current directory, Button1 Save and Button2 Cancel. The verified RC resource identifies these classes; list/key/double-click details remain the platform dialog procedure domain. Modern Common Item Dialog drives S03/S04 at the selection-result boundary.
* Native menu/accelerator controls: all FDxx gameplay entries map to the catalog, not a new command implementation. Win16 Ctrl+A Save As and Ctrl+L Load are historical aliases, not required modern text-field shortcuts; system-menu/extra FDA0-FDAC commands stay reference/compatibility scope. F1/right-button help differences are C-HELP.

### Unresolved component semantics are explicit

Objects with U01 are not silently completed by their selectable flag. This includes extra edit hit regions without a ProcEditEvent case, auxiliary file/debug regions, some count/label hit areas, and unconfirmed same-slot Win16 behavior. Their exact raw fields are retained; a future implementation must either prove a semantic action or treat them as noninteractive presentation data. The catalog covers their existence without inventing a button. Generic legacy selection feedback may still mutate source UI state and is not automatically a business operation.

The Win16-only ribbon definitions (2200/2300), programmable button chooser2400, native scrollbar/system-menu controls, accelerators and native OPEN/SAVEAS subcontrols are decomposed in the historical ribbon/menu tables retained below. Those functions are reference evidence; unsupported Win16 state is not imported into DOS.

# Action catalog: reinvent controls around canonical operations

This catalog is the implementation authority. Component/object IDs below are evidence locating behavior, not a prescription to build one native widget per old Obj. A modern control issues an action ID plus semantic parameters. Its adapter either enters an existing complete logic routine at the game pump or drives a retained source transaction where that logic remains interaction-coupled. **No native callback writes game globals or reproduces game rules.**

Routes: **L** = candidate complete canonical logic entry (requires adapter proof before use); **E** = original event/held-input path required until a complete entry is established; **T** = original nested menu/dialog/result transaction retained; **H** = host-only operation with zero canonical writes; **U** = unresolved, do not invent an action. None of L/E/T is a claim that a new native adapter has already been verified. Reads/writes name the semantic authorities and important effects, not an exhaustive machine-write census; all transitive canonical effects remain required.

Each action is evaluated at an agreed source pump seam, with an explicit deterministic logical-owner preparation independent of native layout/focus. Stateful gestures carry an ordered parameter/sample/modifier transcript and a begin/commit/cancel state. Same final slider value or tile alone is insufficient when the source reacts to intermediate samples. Host layout/search/zoom operations are a separate action class and cannot create a gameplay transcript.

Canonical source locations: session/dirty lifecycle `src/startup/game_session.c`; menu/pause `src/ui/game_menu.c`; window gates/feedback `src/window/events.c` and `lifecycle.c`; world/player `src/ui/yellow_commands.c` and `src/simulation/life_tiles.c`; navigation/yard placement `src/ui/view_commands.c`; map/minimap `src/ui/map_window.c`, `minimap.c`; allocation `src/ui/colony_controls.c`; tools `src/ui/editor_menus.c`, `experiment_menu.c`, `experiment_tools.c`; information/history `src/ui/info_cards.c`, `history_window.c`; score/help/dialogs `src/ui/scenario_dialogs.c`; files `src/ui/save_load.c`; special keys `src/ui/game_events.c`, `cheats.c`.

Native Settings panel; no canonical CFG rewrite for display/layout. | **H** Host postmix/settings services; source option changes separately S08-S13 | No alternate source audio schedule or pause policy | Read: Host gain/settings; source commands read-only. Change: Postmix/native preferences only. | Music/effects enable are game actions, not gain. Separate volume stems need independent proof. |
## Unresolved

| Action / reinvented control | Canonical entry and route | Preconditions / modal context | State read -> state changed | Logic coupled to old interaction; exact driving rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="action-u01"></a>**U01 Unexplained/selectable resource regions**. Represent known visual/static data only. Block any proposed interactive replacement until code/original observation resolves its action. | **U** Decoded Obj and consumer default branches; no established action | Unknown; no safe new gameplay precondition can be asserted | Read: Raw resource fields; state only where caller proves it. Change: Unestablished beyond generic selection/feedback effects. | A selectable bit does not establish a business action. Unknown objects must stay explicit; no guessed buttons. |

These are parameter variants of catalog K02/K03 and P-actions, not additional native widget requirements. They preserve shipped input behavior only in an explicit game-input context. Native text fields, docking and command search never enter these paths.

# Workspace, panel instances and logical-window ownership

## Concrete docking model

The main application window is the **workspace container**, like an image editor. Its background is neutral native workspace chrome, not an integer-scaled DOS screen. The primary World panel normally occupies the central document area; it is one panel among others and can be tabbed, split beside inspectors, floated, collapsed or hidden. Native focus and panel layout never select a game plane or raise a source logical window.

The host owns a layout tree with three node kinds: `Split(axis, ratio, first, second)`, `Tabs(panel IDs, active panel)`, and `Float(owned native host, subtree, bounds)`. Four outer docking regions surround a central document region. Each region may contain nested splits and tab groups; a panel is not restricted to its default region. Collapsed groups are represented by edge tabs with a remembered return path. This is host data, not a mirror of `win_offsets`, object rectangles or `clip_window_z_order`.

Default **Play** layout (proposed dimensions are device-independent pixels, not source pixels):

```text
Native workspace frame / menu / command search
+----------------------+-------------------------------+--------------------+
| Ant / World Controls | Central document group        | Overview           |
| plane, targets,      | [World] [optional inspectors] | [Minimap] [Info]   |
| Pause / speed,       |                               |                    |
| world/map layers,    |                               | [Behavior] [Caste] |
| tools and warnings   |                               | optional tab group |
+----------------------+-------------------------------+--------------------+
| Colony Operations: View modes, Mate, queens, placement, colony summaries |
+------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Collapsed/optional [History] [Status] [Population] [Messages] [Tools]     |
+------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

The two command panels are pinned by default, but neither owns the Overview/Minimap or the game world. **Colony** preset brings Behavior/Caste/Population/Status alongside World. **Research** splits World and a read-only inspector, opens Info and History. **Minimal** leaves World and compact command groups, with other panels in edge tabs. **Reset** restores Play on the current monitor. Applying any preset is H03: it cannot call NewGame, select a plane, change Pause, open engine windows or reinitialize game controls.

### Drag, split, stack and float

* Drag from a native panel title/tab after an 8-DIP threshold. An overlay shows workspace-edge targets and target-group center/edge targets. Center stacks as a tab; an edge splits that group; an outer target docks against the workspace edge. A preview rectangle and accessible text name the result before release. Escape cancels without changes.
* Splitter hit areas are 6 DIP with keyboard focus. Arrow keys adjust the split by a small increment; Shift uses a larger increment; Home restores equal split. Ratios are clamped by children’s minimum sizes, and applied only to native geometry.
* Dropping outside creates an **owned** native floating host containing the panel or dragged tab group. Floating hosts participate in the one application's activation/minimization and taskbar grouping. Their close button hides that native group, without any `win_Close`. Dragging a group back to a target restores docking.
* Titles provide Move Panel, Stack With, Split, Float, Collapse, Hide and Reset Group commands, so docking never requires precision dragging. Each command changes the same layout tree. Controls within a panel reflow between horizontal and vertical arrangements; they are not scaled old button rows.
* A source-owned brush/allocation/placement gesture must finish normally before a dock drag begins. Defer or disable the layout drag during that gesture; do not synthesize a source mouse-up/cancel to make docking work. Ordinary passive tab/layout changes during a source wait must not steal its semantic owner or service a new gameplay command.

### Minimum sizes and small displays

| Panel family | Proposed minimum content size (DIP) | Overflow/reflow |
|---|---|---|
| Primary World / world inspector | 320×240; prefer at least 480×320 | More native client area shows more world; viewport zoom/fit is independent of canonical geometry. No world scrollbars. |
| Overview / Minimap | 200×140 / 160×100 | Aspect-fit thumbnail, labelled layers and accessible camera target; controls move to native overflow menu. |
| Ant / World Controls | 220×150 vertical or 380×72 horizontal | Groups wrap or collapse into labelled native command menus, without hiding active tool state. |
| Colony Operations | 320×110 | Wrap mode/queen groups; retain counts and active placement indicator. |
| Behavior / Caste | 240×200 | Labelled allocation values/presets; native internal scrolling only for controls/text, never world navigation. |
| History | 320×180 | Plot and legend reflow; switch to accessible table at narrow width. |
| Info / Help / Inspect | 260×180 | Normal native text/image scrolling and text scale. |
| Status / Population / Tools / Messages | 220×140 | Reflow to a list/table; normal content scroll where appropriate. |
| Prompt / New Game / Results | 320×180 initially, content-driven | Source-critical choices remain visible; native text scroll if necessary. |

Suggested workspace minimum is 720×480 DIP, with an initial size around 1120×760 clamped to the current work area. On smaller work areas or increased system text scale, collapse lower-priority side groups into recoverable edge tabs rather than shrinking text or changing the engine viewport. Keep an active source Prompt visible. Sizes are implementation defaults to validate with actual content, not claimed historical geometry. Native control minimums grow with font/text scale.

### Keyboard and accessibility

F6/Shift+F6 cycles visible panel groups; Ctrl+Tab cycles tabs only while a workspace group, not a text editor, handles the chord. Floating groups are reachable through native Window menu and app-owned window cycling. Every panel has a keyboard Move Panel menu with named targets, Float/Redock and Collapse/Restore. A native Close/Hide-panel command never sends the original game-window-close event. Focus returns to the last native control in a revealed tab, without canonical activation.

All title bars, tabs, splitters, command groups, custom canvases, allocation editors and charts need UI Automation names/roles/values/actions and visible native focus feedback. A custom view is not accessible merely because it has a native HWND. Tooltips, selected text, search, plot hover and additional accessible numeric descriptions are H06 operations; source actions remain the catalog's gameplay commands.

### Persistence, DPI and monitors

Save a versioned host layout document containing panel type/instance IDs, split tree/ratios, tab order and selection, collapsed return paths, floating group bounds in DIP, last monitor identity/work-area-relative position, native text scale and per-view presentation preferences. Do **not** store or restore `MapPlane`, `MapPnt`, `GamePaused`, `GameSpeed`, source logical-open/front state, caste levels or `.ANT` contents as a workspace layout. A primary view’s presentational zoom can be remembered without applying a gameplay navigation command.

Persist changes on completed docking/layout operations, not each splitter pixel. Validate unknown/missing panel IDs, schema versions and corrupt files; preserve the old file for diagnosis and fall back to Play. Loading a named layout uses one host transaction and restores native focus. A currently active source Prompt is injected as a visible task even if the stored layout hid its panel; it cannot be dropped from the layout tree until resolved.

Use per-monitor-aware native geometry/text and explicit logical-to-device transforms for custom views. On DPI changes, recompute native metrics and render targets; on monitor removal clamp floats into an available work area and retain a reachable caption. Reconnection can restore a preferred arrangement, but never a source game mode. Never persist raw physical screen coordinates alone. Mixed-DPI, hot-unplug, Remote Desktop, maximize/restore and taskbar edge cases require hardware/runtime validation. None is an excuse to resize canonical window records.

## Panel families, instance policy and every inventory role

| Native panel family | Instances | Source relationship / interaction policy |
|---|---|---|
| World (patch/nest/yard/house/strategic/graphic) | **One interactive primary**; up to **three additional read-only inspectors** | Primary actions affect the one canonical world/camera/mode. Inspectors have native cameras/zoom/layers and may observe a different plane/yard snapshot only through a pure all-plane reader. Current-plane-only `world_view` must be extended before other-plane inspectors are enabled. No temporary writes to global MapPlane/MapPnt. |
| Overview and Minimap | One each, independently dockable | Both are real panels, not just a picture inside a toolbar. Read-only thumbnail views can coexist while source Map/Yard share logical geometry. Center/Go Here is an explicit N04 action; revealing a panel is H01. |
| Behavior, Caste, History, Info/cards, Status, Population, Help, Inspect, Messages, Tools | One authoritative panel of each initially | Can dock, stack, float, collapse or hide. Merely displaying data cannot open the related logical source window or reset its private UI state. Read-only historical/card copies are presentation snapshots, not additional canonical owners. Multiple editable allocation/control panels are deliberately excluded to avoid gesture ownership ambiguity. |
| Ant / World Controls and Colony Operations | One each; dock/float/reflow anywhere | Two command surfaces share catalog action IDs/state. No duplicate canonical widgets, flags or model. Native focus may stay in a command control while the action executes in another logical owner. |
| Ant/queen/spider/scent/context/proximity menus and editor submenus | Views of one native action/Tools context, not persistent copies of each old menu window | Native menus/options expose the catalog's semantics. Source transactions may run hidden where inseparable; no original bitmap menu or original widget is presented. |
| Welcome/title, Scenario/New Game, Results/End Game, About/Credits | One content panel of each, shown for that lifecycle | Native workspace content, floatable/dockable. Starting/accepting source lifecycle actions remains S/I catalog behavior. Source-created request visibility is separate from a user browsing a retained read-only result. |
| Prompt (birth/death/event/tutorial/picture text/continue/errors/save confirmation/file decision) | One active source task; queued source tasks follow source ordering | Source modal context pins and selects its native content. It can be docked or floated, but not hidden/collapsed/buried in another tab while unanswered. Other panels may remain visible/readable; disallowed gameplay commands are blocked. This is the necessary exception to unrestricted panel hiding, not an extra simulation pause. |
| File picker and native host Settings | One active service UI each | Native platform picker at the file-selection service boundary, not a game-window replica. Settings is host UI except explicit S-actions. Both retain source audio/clock/wait policy. |
| Debug and unresolved auxiliary source controls | One optional developer panel | Show understood read-only diagnostics. No new entry/action for unknown original controls; U01/K04 remain explicit debt. |

Creating, focusing or panning an inspector is passive H04. To play at its location, choose **Go Here in Primary**, which issues the normal N01/N02/N04 action sequence with explicit target plane/patch. That command may change game state and RNG; focusing or promoting a tab cannot do it implicitly. First implementation may restrict inspectors to the already published plane until all-plane snapshots are proved; it must not mutate the game to fake another view.

## Deconstructed modern command panels

**Ant / World Controls** presents command groups around player intent: active plane/mode, Go To targets, Pause/speed, player Move/Interact/Recruit/Release/Exchange/Alarm, World Layer and Map Layer separately, Tools mode, health readouts and Alert Below settings. Map/Minimap buttons reveal their panels (H01); Center/Go Here are N-actions. Health values are read-only; warning threshold actions do not heal. Use ordinary native segmented choices, labelled command buttons/menus and accessible numeric controls, with compact overflow. No original control-column silhouette or `Obj`-indexed widget construction.

**Colony Operations** presents view mode, patch transfer, mating, stored queens, automatic/manual placement, Place Queen, colony/population summaries and allocation/History/Info panel reveal commands. Source eligibility controls action availability and validation. The panel remains visible if an action is inapplicable, with a reason derived from the snapshot and source result; showing it must not select Yard or change plane. Entering a queen-placement transaction belongs to Y04, not panel activation.

**Behavior/Caste** are separate allocation editors. Their modern shape is not constrained to the old triangle: recommend three labelled live values, two independent adjustable dimensions, presets and Auto/Manual. The backend still uses the original integer conversion/commit logic. Until a complete parameterized allocation boundary is proved, expose only reachable source results through the retained held-input transaction; show the actual accepted values and quantization. A native ternary visualization is optional. Do not implement a second normalizer or change `IdealCaste` directly.

**History/Info/Status/Help** are native data/text tools with their own panel layouts. Opening a native panel does not execute the original “open window” command. Actual source series/card/help/score operations are explicit I/Y-actions; independent native browsing/hover/read-only inspection is H06/I02. This distinction must be visible in command naming where it matters: Reveal Status is layout; Calculate Source Status or answering an active status prompt is a source transaction.

## Logical windows are an engine state machine, not the docking tree

The source owns all `win_*` state and its effects. The host owns native focus/layout. A third, narrow **action executor** owns the temporary semantic lease that lets one action use a source logical owner. No bidirectional synchronization of the two trees is permitted.

| Source state / operation | Relation to native panel operations | Required handling |
|---|---|---|
| NewGame/Load initialize and open source default views | Independent of chosen workspace preset | Let original SetDefaultWindows/initializations execute. In normal setup Edit0000, Map/Yard context and Behavior1200/Caste1300 are opened by source routines; the exact order/front comes from those routines. Do not open all inventory windows at startup for native panels. |
| Edit0000 logically open | Enables UpdateEdit and its coupled effects | Preserve it while source gameplay keeps it open, even if every native world panel is hidden or covered. If source lifecycle explicitly closes it, respect that state; do not force it open from a repaint. |
| Map0100 versus Yard1900 | Canonical win_Swap/shared transform, one active game context | Native Overview/Minimap/yard inspectors can all remain visible using independent snapshots/transforms. Only N/Y actions perform source transitions or prepare the corresponding owner. |
| Behavior1200/Caste1300 logical open and cleanup | Original handlers/animation sets need source state/geometry | Source action preparation ensures needed owner/state at a safe seam. Hiding/collapsing the native editor never calls win_ModeControlClosed/win_CasteControlClosed or clears their source animation handles. |
| History1500 / Info0500 / Status1800 and other optional logical windows closed | Native read-only panel may still be shown | Read buffers/immutable card data/last valid computed result without opening source window. Source Info first-open initializes its ring, so a real card action includes that preparation. Source ScoreDialog writes OverallScore, so do not run it to keep a panel fresh. A pure CalcScore call with native-local output needs a no-write audit; otherwise show a timestamped last result. |
| Source menus/dialogs/waits open temporary logical windows | Native task/menu can be a different visual shape | Preserve the exact source lifecycle/flush/tracker/result effects hidden; keep a task token. Do not interpret a docking operation or native menu disappearance as source acceptance/cancellation. |
| Source win_ToTop/win_Open and front-gated events | Not triggered by native tab/focus/OS activation | Only an explicit catalog action requiring that owner performs preparation. The preparation is the same for the same action and canonical initial state in every layout. |
| Source close/auto-dismiss/lower/zoom/move/resize | May alter stack, geometry, resources and drawing | Execute only when source lifecycle or an explicit compatibility action requests it. Native panel close/move/split/resize never forwards to these operations. |
| Source-driven modal task | Owns event eligibility even if native focus is elsewhere | Route only accepted task input; restore/show Prompt from any layout. Source actual waits/timing remain authoritative. |

### Deterministic action execution and canonical front

Every gameplay intent is `(action ID, semantic parameters, ordered gesture samples, modifiers, task token, source seam)`. Panel IDs, native HWNDs, tab order, focus history and window size are **not gameplay parameters**. A pending queue outside the source event queue retains an intent until it can be delivered once.

Docking drags, native text input, tab shortcuts, hover and panel focus do not leak into the guest mouse/keyboard state. Only the currently authorized source action/transaction receives a virtual canonical pointer/key/modifier transcript through the existing input boundary. Otherwise an innocent dock hover could trigger source edge scrolling or a Ctrl shortcut. If app deactivation interrupts a held gameplay gesture, its source release/cancel is an explicit recorded gesture outcome; it is not silently classified as passive layout. Moving focus between owned panels/floats never creates that outcome.

For L-actions, use the existing complete canonical routine at a safe source pump only after its preconditions, implicit globals, cleanup and transitive effects have been audited. A function being callable or well named is not sufficient proof. Do not add an unnecessary source window raise to an L-action that does not require front. For E/T-actions, perform a deterministic action-specific preparation: open/raise the required logical owner if necessary, let `win_Open` finish its queue flush and pointer-region setup, then inject the original event/transaction samples. Generic object selection, group effects and double-click bookkeeping still run where the source path needs them. Never invoke gameplay from a native callback while the engine is reentrant or in an incompatible wait.

`win_IsWinInFront` and `win_translate_pending_event` therefore see the owner needed by the **action**, while Windows focus can remain on a native numeric field, toolbar or floating panel. The cached last-rendered `view.top` no longer decides whether to consume another click. A native activation click creates one intent; it is not dropped, replayed speculatively, or sent before the source flush. A source gesture leases its logical owner through completion—especially `processExp` (map-versus-edit coordinate branch) and `PlaceQueenInYard` (front-window cancellation). A layout/focus change cannot change that lease.

Clicks in an enlarged native world normalize first to a world tile. Any required canonical navigation-to-include that tile is computed from **canonical camera/geometry and the semantic target**, never native client size. Record that navigation prelude in the action transcript and verify it. Resizing or an extra view must not add a different number of source scroll/redraw calls. Native zoom/fit/inspection camera movement does not recenter MapPnt; actionable pan remains N03. The old hidden edit geometry stays fixed by source behavior, not by the layout manager.

### What stays running hidden

The canonical source remains untouched. Native assets and semantic snapshots replace every visible VGA component, but effectful original execution still occurs at its original source seams. `UpdateEdit`, DrawSpider/DrawPalps/DrawCurBalloons, original animation/window hooks, input classification, menu/prox loops, myDelay/sound service and dialog waits can mutate RNG/timers/cache/queues. Their private raster output may remain execution support and is never displayed. Do not no-op these routines, invoke them for each native view, or run an extra simulation loop while a native panel paints.

Capture typed semantic outputs (source-selected poses/frames, message text/anchors/deadlines, card/link records, prompt arguments) at their production seam. Native repaints use those snapshots and immutable art without touching source scratch/cache/resource residency. The birth-dialog asset-lifetime fix follows this rule. Passive observers of another plane require pure all-plane decoding from copied data; temporarily changing source globals and restoring them is not allowed.

Copy source time/deadline observations at existing source seams. Do not call TickCount/MacTickCount or other source timing services from a panel paint, tooltip, layout manager or observer just to label a snapshot: extra reads may matter under the pinned machine clock rule. Host animation/presentation time is separate and never advances simulation.

Private static UI state and draw-produced link/animation data are integration proof obligations, not invitations to create shadow game models. Where no clean read/command boundary exists, retain the original source path invisibly and mark the corresponding catalog action E/T. Extracting a stronger boundary later requires source/portability evidence and action-equivalence tests; it never justifies rewriting canonical simulation for the frontend.

# Inventory: original meaning, current implementation, native replacement

The complete shipped DOS census contains **285 objects across logical windows 0x0000–0x2100**. [CONTROL-MAP.md](../build/workers/enhanced-design/CONTROL-MAP.md) and [control-ledger.json](../build/workers/enhanced-design/control-ledger.json) list each object, type, initial flags, native disposition and source event consumer. They remain a historical census; the component deconstruction and action catalog above supersede their earlier one-widget/one-event implementation wording. Initial resource flags do not replace runtime visible/enabled/selected state. Structural objects, palettes, anchor objects and help pictures are data; they are not 285 independent native widgets.

IDs `0x80/0x81/0x83` in the kind-0 database are window-system metadata: counts, palettes and purge policy, not extra game windows (`win_LoadAllWindows`). The host has 45 possible slots; a slot's existence does not prove a shipped user-facing window.

Abbreviations in this historical inventory only: **H** = current native frame with hosted VGA content; **M** = current hybrid modern world plus hosted controls. This inventory H is distinct from the catalog H host-only action route. Mac mappings beyond named resources are marked as inferred; a lack of a WIND resource does not prove absence of a code-created popup.

| Game role / DOS IDs | DOS presentation and behavior | Win16 reference | Mac reference | Current / screenshot | Native replacement; reads and source actions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Game/Edit `0000` | World viewport `0004/0015`; left controls, health/warning bars, grow/scroll decorations | GenericWindow child; resize and native scrolling; edit controls retained | WIND 1000 “Edit Wind”; CNTL 1000/1001 scroll controls | M; [Quick](../build/workers/enhanced-design/r2/quick/frame.bmp.0000.png), [larger/zoomed](../build/workers/enhanced-design/r2/zoom/frame.bmp.0000.png) | Normally central workspace World panel, plus optional read-only inspectors; immutable art + Map/Life/pheromone/player snapshot. No inner frame or scrollbars. Actions retain `processEdit`, `ProcEditEvent` and canonical camera paths. |

# Historical control and ribbon evidence retained for deconstruction

## What Win16 actually did

`InitApplication` registers AntRoot, GenericWindow and RibbonWindow. The original-code analysis in `D:/Prog/simantw_recon/evidence/port/sections/ANSWERS.md` establishes normal logical windows as **WS_CHILD of rootWnd**, not independent top-level windows and not the Windows MDI framework. The ribbon is a special child of the main frame. Logical **0x2200 and 0x2300 share one HWND and are swapped with context**; `ribbonBarHeight` is zero in the inspected release. There are two ribbon *definitions*, not proof of two simultaneously visible bars. The edit window also retains its own control column.

The new design deliberately makes two independently dockable panels from these related roles, including the edit column's controls. That is the owner's modern organization, not a claim to reproduce the old layout.

The exact reference entry points are:

* `src/recovered/ProcMapRibbonEvent-8ce2938669.c` (Win16).
* `evidence/recovery/drafts/ProcYardRibbonEvent/76c0687fe5b7.c` (preserved draft; hypotheses in private data remain explicit).
* `src/recovered/wf_tu_antedit_C19C_InitMapFunctions_4_scaffold-619861a01a.c::DrawMapData` and `wf_tu_antedit_A998_DrawYardData_2_scaffold-2f57d5cc12.c::DrawYardData`. Use their actual named bodies; adjacent pool stubs are **not** recovered logic.
* `src/recovered/tu_antedit_00F4_OverlayTileSet_22_reviewed-c56efa1dba.c::ProcEditEvent` and the DoUserButton/DoBookMark drafts and original symbol positions.

Do not pass Win16 `0x22xx/0x23xx` events to DOS: DOS `DoEvent` does not dispatch them. Resolve the player intent to a catalog action whose complete DOS logic/path is established. Host conveniences remain H-actions; no old-widget binding scheme is prescribed.

## Historical components contributing to Ant / World Controls

This is the source/reference affordance trace, retained to explain original intent. It is **not** the new panel specification or a binding list for new widgets. The workspace section defines reinvented controls around the action catalog; Overview and Minimap are separate panels. Where a candidate complete action entry is proven, it can supersede an original widget event at the adapter boundary.

| Map/minimap, DOS `0005`, Win16 `2206` | MapMode, plane, overview transform and camera bounds | `0005 -> OpenMiniMapWin`; overview `0102 -> MapAreaEvent` | Map click/drag centers through original routines; no direct MapPnt assignment. |
| Surface/Black/Red, edit `0008/09/0A`; Win16 `2205/03/04` | MapPlane / selected source objects | `0008/09/0A` or exact corresponding `0106/07/08` action | Plane-specific saved centers, scent button clearing where the selected source path does it. These two entry paths are not silently interchangeable. |

Win16 additionally synchronizes ELayerMode and forces edit update; DOS map button alone does not. Keep scope explicit. |

Warning thresholds are action P11. A native Alert Below control may use an audited parameterized logic entry or a source-reachable value mapping into the retained event path. The old meter shape is not required. Do not assume every integer percentage was reachable in the original interaction, and never turn a threshold into a heal action.

## Complete existing menu command bindings

This table records existing source command equivalents. Reinvented native commands are catalog actions; the table is not a rule to construct widgets from old menu strings. Source equivalents read menu enabled/checked/text state (`g_6054`, updated by `SetMenuEntries`/`SetMenuItemState`) and enqueue `FDxx` through the existing menu boundary. New menu organization and native focus are independent. Explicit catalog game actions retain these source meanings; native Reveal Panel does not issue an original Open Window command. Separators are native styling with no event.

| Original menu | Items -> event codes -> canonical consumer |
|---|---|
| File | About `FD01 -> AboutDialog`; New `FD03 -> NewGame`; Load `FD04`, Save `FD05`, Save As `FD06`, Quit `FD08 -> ui_pending_menu_item -> DoEvent -> LoadGame/SaveGame/MenuQuit`. |
| Window | Edit `FD11 -> OpenEditWindow`; Map `FD12 -> OpenMapYard`; Behavior `FD13 -> OpenModeWindow`; Caste `FD14 -> OpenCasteWindow`; History `FD15 -> OpenHistoryWindow`; Info `FD16 -> OpenInfoWindow`; Status `FD17 -> ScoreDialog`. |
| View | Yard/House/Strategic/Graphic `FD21/22/23/24 -> SetYardMode` and source plane/window transition; Surface/Black Nest/Red Nest `FD26/27/28 -> SetMapPlane`. |
| Options | AutoTrack/Music/Effects/Events/Messages/Silly `FD31/32/33/34/35/36 -> ProcMenu`, original OptionStates toggles and StopSong behavior. |
| Speed | Pause/Unpause `FD41 -> SetPause`; Slow/Normal/Fast/Ultra `FD43/44/45/46 -> GameSpeed` and `SetMenuEntries`. |

New Settings, Reset Layout, dock/float/hide controls, text zoom and native help search are H-actions and enqueue no gameplay command. Closing the application still requests the original Quit transaction; closing a source-owned dialog still sends its original cancellation/continue action. Native Window-menu visibility actions must distinguish a presentation dock toggle from an actual original game-window open/close command; the labels and acceptance traces must not blur them.
